b. Presentation to Valerie Scott on her Retirement from Navajo County after
23+ Years of Service:
Eric Scott recognized Valerie Scott on her retirement from Navajo County
after over twenty-three years with the Navajo County Jail.  He stated she
has always been a pleasure to work with, that she is very pleasant, kind,
and a great employee. 
Commander Ernie Garcia spoke about Ms. Scott’s work history with the
Jail, indicating that she started as a Detention Deputy and moved into the
civilian ranks throughout her career.  He stated that it will be very hard to
replace her and that she has been a wonderful employee.  He further
stated that she will be missed and wished her the best in her retirement.  
Valerie Scott stated she has enjoyed her work with the County, but that
she will enjoy being able to spend time with her family. 
Vice-Chair Benally congratulated Ms. Scott on her retirement.
 
  
c. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of the amended   
                                                                    2 of 13 
DRAFT

<!-- page 9 -->
c. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of the amended
ordinance, Ordinance Number 03-11, amending Section 2 to increase the
Justice Court Staffing Fee from $30 to $48 effective July 1, 2024 :
Jason Vowell stated that during the budget process they go through and
analyze the County’s funds and accounting system.  He indicated that
during that process it came to their attention that the Ordinance for the
Justice Court Staffing Fee has not been updated since 2012, and that
according to the Ordinance this should have been reviewed annually, but
that this has not been done.   He stated that as part of the budgeting
process they look at the activity and the revenue that is received in the
fund to help offset some of the costs that the County incurs for doing the
service, which supports eight clerical and attorney positions throughout
the County.  He indicated that the proposed increase from $30.00 to
$48.00 is significant, but that it has not been increased since 2012, and
that it should have been done year over year incrementally.  He spoke
about the process that they used to determine the increase, indicating that
they have used the same process for other increases when looking at
inflationary measures.  He stated that the increase still doesn’t cover the
entire cost, that it isn’t meant to cover entire cost, and that they do
transfer from the general fund to support this and that even after the
proposed increase the general fund will cover approximately 40%.  He
indicated that he reviewed the numbers from 2012 and that at that time
the general fund supported between eight and then percent and that the
general fund is taking on more of the burden year after year.  He stated
that staff is recommending adoption of the ordinance to increase the fee
and that going forward they will take a more active approach at looking at
it annually.   
Vice-Chair Benally opened the meeting for public comments. 
Melissa Buckley stated that there were two emails received from Former
Supervisor Jerry Brownlow and Former Constable Ryan Reinhold and
that copies have been provided to the Board.  Ms. Buckley read the
comments from both individuals regarding their concerns about the
proposed increase of fees. 
A member of the public inquired what the historical increases were prior
to this per year.
Mr. Vowell stated that he doesn’t have the exact amount but that it was
not as much of as increase as the amount being recommended. 
Brad Carlyon clarified that his office did not ask for the increase and that
he was unaware that this helped fund his office.
There being no more comments, the public hearing was closed. 
Mr. Vowell answered questions of the Board regarding the functions and
activities that are supported with the fund, the number of positions it
impacts, how the amount of the proposed increase was determined, and
finding a possible compromise. 
Supervisor Seymore stated that there are many things in the budget
they do to try and make sure that things are cost effective and the
activities going forward can pay for themselves.  He indicated that there
have also been some costs that were appropriated from the State and that
the County will be sharing some increase of those, that have to do with
  
                                                                    3 of 13 
DRAFT

<!-- page 10 -->
court costs.    He indicated that he is okay with the increase and spoke of
the reasons that the costs are assessed.  He stated that this should have
been looked at sooner but that they are trying to bring it up to date.   
Brad Carlyon spoke about the types of cases that the County Attorney’s
Office handles in the Justice Courts.  He indicated that the cost also
assists the Public Defender’s Office.  He further indicated that he is not
sure how it will impact impartiality of the Justice Court’s due to the
routinely imposing mandatory fines, fees and surcharges.
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to adopt the amended ordinance,
Ordinance Number 03-11, amending Section 2 to increase the Justice
Court Staffing Fee from $30 to $45 effective July 1, 2024, and that the
ordinance be reviewed annually, motion seconded
by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion passed 4-0 with Chairman Whiting 
being absent.

REGULAR AGENDA:
 
1. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Ordinance Number
01-24, repealing all other ordinances concerning animal care and control
and adopting this comprehensive Ordinance:
Rhonda Krouse, Code Enforcement Division Manager, stated that she
oversees three programs, one of them being Animal Program, and
that Brandon Damron is the Program Manager that oversees the
day-to-day operations.  A presentation was shown.  Ms. Krouse indicated
that in reviewing the programs ordinances and contracts the team
determined that they needed to update the terminology, definitions and
standardization of the ordinance. She further indicated that there were
multiple ordinances in the program that they were able to combine into
one, that they were able to align them more with the Arizona Revised
Statutes and to reflect some of the issues that they are currently
experiencing.  She stated that the number one goal of the Animal Care
  
                                                                    4 of 13 
DRAFT

<!-- page 11 -->
and Control Program is to educate the public and to provide awareness
on the issues that they run into.  She spoke about the proposed
modifications and additions to the ordinances, including adding definitions
for aggressive, attack, County Enforcement Agent, and Cruelty; adding
new ordinances for keeping of wild or exotic animals, keeping of horses,
defined quarantine animals/area, Hearing Officer, and added penalties
from Arizona Revised Statutes.  She talked about the proposed
modifications to the fee schedule, noting that they are requesting a
moderate increase due to inflation which affects market forces for dog
food, gas, equipment and vehicle maintenance.  She stated that calls for
dogs at large are approximately sixty percent of their volume and often
leads to conflicts between neighbors and that they anticipate the fee will
play a significant part in reducing the issues.  She further stated that with
the kennel permits will assist with hoarding issues and the overcrowding
in the shelters, indicating that the ones in our area are all at capacity at
this time.
Supervisor Whitesinger inquired as to the dog at large, if it is for a dog
running in the community or due to the conflict with community members,
noting that there is a difference in the amount based on the location.
Ms. Krouse indicated that it is state law and that they govern the
unincorporated areas. She stated that for the unincorporated areas they
have to be on a leash, be a working dog or have to be under voice
control.  She indicated that the municipalities have their own ordinances. 
Vice-Chair Benally opened the meeting for public comments. 
Ken Kato inquired about, based on a previous incident, what ordinances
are there for dogs maiming or killing a citizen. 
Brad Carlyon stated that there are crime statutes for those issues.
Ms. Krouse stated that they can only site for civil issues, not criminal.
Louise Legett, Snowflake, stated that she has a friend with farm dogs and
that people will go by and harass the dogs while they are working, which
causes them to growl.  She indicated that there is right to farm laws that
protect them and inquired if the right to farm laws are incorporated in this
ordinance.  She stated that her friend has had trouble with the courts, but
her dogs are doing their job. 
Ms. Krouse stated that you have to be in voice control of your animal, or
they have to be working dogs, which is covered. 
There being no additional comments, the public hearing was closed.  
Supervisor Seymore inquired if this ordinance will be stronger than the
ordinances that the municipalities have.    
Ms. Krouse indicated that it would not.  She stated that they looked at
other ordinances within Navajo County and that they tried to keep it
neutral and that she doesn’t feel that they have trumped any of the other
ordinances.
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt Ordinance Number
01-24, repealing all other ordinances concerning animal care and control
and adopting this comprehensive Ordinance, motion seconded
by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion passed 4-0 with Chairman Whiting 
being absent.

PUBLIC WORKS:
 
a. Consideration of a Land Purchase Agreement for additional Right-of-Way
along Bourdon Ranch Road for drainage purposes:
A presentation was shown.  William Flake stated that the Bourdon Ranch
Road at Millet Swale has experienced some unsafe stormwater runoff
due to the failure of the dam that is upstream of the roadway.  He
indicated that the County is working on a capital improvement project,
which includes increasing the capacity of the two culverts on Bourdon
Ranch Road.  He stated that the County has the necessary easements to
move forward with the project that they anticipate to begin in early
August.  He indicated that the property owner has chosen to deed
additional right of way to Navajo County for drainage purposes, and that
the signing of the agreement will grant Navajo County the title over the
property for maintenance and reconstruction of the culvert.  He indicated
that the purchase of the right of way would be for the amount of
$1800.00.  He stated that staff recommends approval.  He addressed the
location, noting that there are two culverts and that the right of way would
be at the first culvert at the south end. 
 
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to approve a Land Purchase
Agreement for additional Right-of-Way along Bourdon Ranch Road for
drainage purposes, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion
passed 4-0 with Chairman Whiting being absent.
 
  
b. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Ordinance Number
02-2024, approving the removal of motor vehicles, trailers, and storage
containers from County-maintained rights-of-way during snow season and
for the enforcement of parking restrictions by issuance of citations and/or
towing :
Cody Cooper stated that this is for a snow winter parking ordinance that
would be in effect from November 1 to April 1.  He stated that the intent of
this ordinance is to prevent the parking of vehicles, trailers, and storage
containers within the county maintained right of way during typical snow
season, noting that during snow season the vehicles can delay or prevent
the ability to maintain and do snow removal on roadways and that this
would offer a mechanism for law enforcement to tow for the offense.  He
spoke about the fines that could be imposed for the offenses, indicating
that it is discretionary and that they will see if it inhibits the maintenance. 
He stated that he worked with Public Works and Sheriff’s Office staff on
the language that everyone would be comfortable with, but also to make
sure that staff are able to do safe and timely maintenance of the roads
during winter snow season.  He indicated that staff has not received any
comments regarding this ordinance. 
 
Vice-Chair Benally opened the meeting for public comments.  There
being no comments, the public hearing was closed.
  
                                                                    6 of 13 
DRAFT

<!-- page 13 -->
being no comments, the public hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Whitesinger inquired if this is specific to snow season and
not for other types of complaints for parking.   
Mr. Cooper stated that this is specific to the normal snow season, from
November to April.  
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt Ordinance Number
02-2024, approving the removal of motor vehicles, trailers, and storage
containers from County-maintained rights-of-way during snow season and
for the enforcement of parking restrictions by issuance of citations and/or
towing, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion passed 4-0
with Chairman Whiting being absent.
 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT: 
 
1. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number
22-2024, approving/denying a Zone Change from R-2 to
Commercial-Residential (C-R) on an approximately 0.33-acre portion of
an approximately 2.28-acre parcel, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow
Lake Drive:
A presentation a shown.  Tyler Richards stated that it is a single parcel
approximately 2.28 acres in size, and that the zone change request is for
.33 portion of that parcel.  He further stated that it is owned by Rim
Country RV Park, that there is currently an existing 38-space RV park,
that it is a legal non-conforming use of the property, that the applicant
plans to bring the parcels into compliance for the expansion, and that if
the zone change is granted, they will move forward with a request for a
special use permit.  He reviewed the location of the parcel, indicating that
it is consistent with usage in the area and that it is zoned C-R.  He spoke
about the proposed site plan.  He stated that staff recommends approval
and that the Planning and Zoning Commission has also unanimously
recommended approval.
Vice-Chair Benally opened the meeting for public comments.  There
being no comments, the Public Hearing was closed. 
 
Supervisor Whitesinger thanked all those who have been involved in
this process.   
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
22-2024, approving a Zone Change from R-2 to Commercial-Residential
(C-R) on an approximately 0.33-acre portion of an approximately
2.28-acre parcel, located in Lakeside, along Rainbow Lake Drive, motion
seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion passed 4-0 with Chairman
Whiting being absent.

2. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number
23-2024, approving/denying a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park
with 38 existing spaces and an expansion to add another 28 spaces, for a
total of 66 spaces:
A presentation was shown.  Tyler Richards stated that this is a special
use permit to expand a thirty-eight space RV Park for an additional
twenty-eight spaces, for a total of sixty-six spaces.  He indicated that
there is an existing six foot sold fence around the property, that they will
improve emergency access along Rainbow Lake Drive, that they will
extend the gravel driveway for access to the new lots, and that they plan
on including open and undeveloped areas.  He spoke about the location
of the property, the layout of the site plan, and the public access points. 
He stated that this project meets numerous portions and is in compliance
with the comprehensive plan, as well as the zoning ordinance, that it has
been properly posted and noticed in compliance with state law, that it is
compatible with zoning uses for that area and that it will have minimal
adverse effects of the general public health, safety and general welfare.
 He further stated that staff recommends approval and that the Planning
and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval, subject to
the sixteen conditions that were listed.
Vice-Chair Benally opened the meeting for public comments. 
Paul Betz, Lakeside, spoke about the access from the development being
White Mountain Blvd. and inquired if it would be less of a hassle for traffic
to exit onto Rainbow Lake Road, with a possible traffic light.
There being no additional comments, the Public Hearing was closed.
 
Supervisor Whitesinger inquired if there has been any opposition
received to this item.
Mr. Richards stated that there have not been any comments for or
against the item.
Supervisor Whitesinger talked about concerns with traffic and inquired
how they account for traffic with the significant increase in spaces.
Mr. Richards stated that there are two conditions that will require them to
comply with all vision site triangle rules and that there is a condition that
requires them to keep the area clear near the 260 and not block visibility. 
He indicated that traffic control measures are not something feasible for
the project, that with it being off the 260 if would bring concern with it
being Arizona Department of Transportation and that it was not
something that looked at or felt would be necessary for the project
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about the concerns with increased
population and the challenges of being able to get around the community.
She stated that the partnership is important to be able to think about
resolutions and be proactive at looking at the roadways with the increased
population.
Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to adopt Resolution Number
23-2024, approving a Special Use Permit to permit an RV Park with 38
existing spaces and an expansion to add another 28 spaces, for a total of
66 spaces, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion passed
4-0 with Chairman Whiting being absent.

COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney

Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about the service work done at the Pinetop

CALL TO THE PUBLIC:
Vice-Chair Benally stated that comments are limited to three minutes per person,
that the Board can’t dialogue, discuss, consider or decide any issues raised during
Call to the Public but can respond to criticism or may direct staff to review an item. 
She stated that professional decorum is expected, that civil demeanor needs to be
maintained, including respect of others, and that speakers who choose to violate
the guidelines will be given a warning and then asked to leave if the conduct
continues.  She further stated that speakers are asked to refrain from repeating
comments or questions already shared, noting that residents are welcome to share
additional concerns with the Board of Supervisors outside of Call to the Public by
contacting the Clerk of the Board. 
Danielle Earl, Linden, spoke about the I-40 Trade Port Corridor, noting that she is
speaking not only as a resident of Navajo County, but on behalf of the residents of
Winslow who would be affected by this.  She stated that she is providing
documents for the Board to review, including fourteen letters from residents, the
parcel summary report and unofficial warranty deeds for the nine properties owned
by Atlas Global Development Group, Inc.   She requested that the Board not
approve the annexation of the properties to the City of Winslow.  She spoke about
the development agreement that is on the Winslow City Councils agenda.  She
indicated as part of the trade port plan they want to have a bypass route diverting
truck traffic from I-40 toward Highway 87, which is the stated reasoning for the
application of the Raised Grant.  She indicated that Atlas Global has been buying
property in the area in hopes of developing an industrial mega site, noting that they
describe the existing site character as primarily vacant land with interspersed
residential parcels.  She indicated that the future of the area is to build hotels,
hangers, light industrial commerce park and multi family housing, but that the area
would need to be annexed to the city and zoned commercial to fulfill the plan.  She
further indicated that the issue is the interspersed residential parcels standing in
the way, that the residents of those parcels are protesting the annexation and the
                                                                    10 of 13

<!-- page 17 -->
types of companies they’re trying to attract, because they will lose their rights and
property to further the agenda of the project.  She requested that the property rights
of the owners be protected, and that the property not be annexed to the city. 
Gordon Lange, Navajo County, stated that he, as well as other citizens, have
concerns about election integrity and the election machines.  He spoke about the
issues he had with the machine when he voted, indicating that he understands
others had similar issues.  He indicated that it should be a hand count with
observers or cameras observing the integrity of the election.  He spoke about his
concerns with the machines due to the potential for being hacked.   
Donna Shurwin, Lakeside, spoke about the Citizens Clean Election Act and the
purpose of it being to root out corruption and promote confidence and participation
in the political process.  She thanked the Board for getting rid of the DS200’s.  She
talked about concerns with continuing to use the machines at Elections Central. 
She spoke about concerns with the continued use of the drop boxes, indicating that
they should be eliminated, since they need to be monitored and managed 24/7 by
two people, one from each party, and addressed the amount of money it would
save the county.  She spoke about the hand count manual published and accepted
by the State of Missouri.  She stated that there isn’t a lot of confidence in out
county right now.  She spoke of the issue that occurred in Whiteriver during the
Presidential Preference Election.  She stated that she hopes that the Board will
consider returning to hand counting.
Paul Betz, Lakeside, stated that he has concerns with the voting machines. Noting
that the interest in hand counting is starting to spread.  He stated that he
has registered to get involved with the election process, indicating that it is
important to have trust in the system.  He indicated that trust in America has
dropped and that there is too much that goes through the internet where it could be
comprised.  He stated that a hand count is the better way to go. 
Jerry Hubbard, Overgaard, talked about the ballot drop boxes, indicating that they
are not covered in the Arizona Revised Statutes, Section 16.  He spoke about the
requirement for the drop boxes to be monitored as set forth in the Elections
Procedure Manual, noting that when County Manager Bryan Layton asked the
Secretary of State about it, his reply was that there is no requirement for them to be
monitored, indicating that it is a direct contradiction.  He inquired how the
contradiction can be reconciled, indicating that since it affects the Navajo County
Elections, it would be obvious to ask the Sheriff’s Office, which they will do.  He
stated that if elections were done with paper ballots, in-person voting on election
day and hand counted at each polling place it would eliminate ninety percent of the
Election Procedures Manual, noting that simple is best.  
Belinda Quog, Show Low, stated that with the DS200’s no longer being used there
should be a hand count of all ballots/votes at the polling centers, indicating that the
process is simple, efficient and the count will be finished.  She talked about
problems with machines at Elections Central, indicating that there needs to be a
full security assessment of the machines.  She stated that the County could have
notified the citizens about the Logic and Accuracy test that was done yesterday,
indicating that they found out when calling the Elections Department, but that it
should have been on the website, inquiring where the transparency is at.  She
spoke about her observations from the Logic and Accuracy, noting that a hardwired
system is not secure, the password and USB security is primitive, and that only a
sharpie was used for the test deck but that a ballpoint pen should have also been
used.  She stated that the test only covered process and none of the
vulnerabilities.  She stated that they blindly trust the Secretary of State and ES&S

and that if they wanted to build community trust they would have an independent

and urged that it be a priority of the Board. 
Roger Gorres, Heber, stated that he would provide his evaluation of the Logic and

prompt, courteous, and professional manner when answering the questions he

questions that could not be addressed to his County Supervisor, along with
recommendations for solving the most important concerns that he has.   
Vice-Chair Benally noted that the Board may not respond to individual speakers or
discuss topics discussed during the Call to the Public, but that they can respond to

comments that were made and indicated that they will give them their
consideration.

RESOLUTION ____-2024
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA BOARD OF SUPERVISORS,
DESIGNATING THE CHIEF FISCAL OFFICER FOR OFFICIALLY SUBMITTING
THE FISCAL YEAR 2025 EXPENDITURE LIMITATION REPORT TO THE ARIZONA
AUDITOR GENERAL
WHEREAS A.R.S. §41-1279.07(E) requires each county, city, town, and community college
district to annually provide to the Auditor General by July 31 the name of the Chief Fiscal
Officer the governing body designated to officially submit the current year’s annual expenditure
limitation report (AELR) on the governing body’s behalf; and
WHEREAS Entities must submit an updated form and documentation for any changes in the
individuals designated to file the AELR; and
WHEREAS the Navajo County Board of Supervisors desires to designate Jayson Vowell, as the
County's Chief Fiscal Officer.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF
SUPERVISORS AS FOLLOWS:
 The recitals above are hereby incorporated as if fully set forth herein.
 Jayson Vowell is hereby designated as the County’s Chief Fiscal Officer for purposes of
officially submitting the fiscal year 2025 AELR to the Arizona Auditor General’s Office on
the governing body’s behalf.
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this
9th day of July 2024.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Public Safety Personnel Retirement System
Pension Funding Policy – Fiscal Year 2024-2025
This pension funding policy is done in accordance of ARS § 38-386.01. The following terms
used throughout this policy are defined as:
Unfunded Actuarial Accrued Liability (UAAL) – Is the difference between trust assets
and the estimated future cost of pensions earned by employees.
Annual Required Contribution (ARC) – Is the annual amount required to pay into the
pension funds, as determined through annual actuarial valuations. It is comprised of
two primary components: normal pension cost – which is the estimated cost of pension
benefits earned by employees in the current year; and, amortization of UAAL – which
is the cost needed to cover the unfunded portion of pensions earned by employees in
previous years. The UAAL is collected over a period of time referred to as the
amortization period. The ARC is a percentage of the current payroll.
Funded Ratio – Is a ratio of fund assets to actuarial accrued liability.
The Board formally accepts the assets, liabilities, and current funding ratio of the county’s
PSPRS trust funds as reported by PSPRS, the plan administrator from their June 30, 2023
actuarial valuation, which are detailed below.
Trust Fund Assets
Accrued
Liability
Unfunded Actuarial
Accrued Liability / (Asset) Funded Ratio
Sheriff’s Office $23,081,074 $23,706,119 $625,045 97.36%
Corrections Officers $7,180,268 $6,814,414 ($365,854) 105.37%
Totals $30,261,342 $30,520,533 $259,191 99.15%
Consistent with the PSPRS’ Actuarial Funding Policy, the Board’s PSPRS funding ratio goal
is 100% (fully funded) over a period of 14 years*.

<!-- page 24 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
To achieve this goal, the County issued $16,560,000 of Pledged Revenue Obligations in
October 2021.  In addition, a Pension Contingency Reserve Fund was funded during the
FY22-23 budget process.  The Reserve Fund provides a source of funds from which the
County can make additional contributions that may be required in future years.  The
schedule below indicates the remaining debt service payments of principal and interest
associated with the issuance of the pledged revenue obligations.
* Note: The plan to amortize the UAAL over an extended period of time is conditional on the
accuracy of the actuarial assumptions. These assumptions are updated on an annual basis
and the ARC as well as the amortization period may be adjusted.

Dear David M. Clouse,
On behalf of the Arizona Criminal Justice Commission, it is my pleasure to inform you
that your agency's application has been approved for funding under the FY 2025 Drug,
Gang, and Violent Crime Control Program.
Paragraphs six and seven of the enclosed agreement provide the amount of funding
awarded to your agency and the specific positions that have been funded. Below, please
find additional information that will assist in the administration of your agency's grant
award.
Grant Agreement and Other Required Documents: Please review the grantee
agreement and accompanying instructions as revisions or new requirements may have
been included. Please follow the directions outlined in the Grant Agreement instructions,
as well as instructions received in DocuSign to execute the contract in its entirety with
electronic authorized signatures. Agreements not returned within 90 days of the award
date with authorized signatures may be canceled. Additionally, please refer to the Special
Conditions section of the grant agreement for a listing of other required documents, as
applicable.
Administrative and Financial Requirements: In addition to applicable uniform
administrative requirements and cost principles, award recipients are required to adhere to
grant specific program requirements as defined in the grant agreement.
Reporting: Activity and financial reports are required for this grant and the reporting
schedules are contained in the agreement. Financial Reporting can be accessed at: 
https://grantsportal.azcjc.gov/. Activity reporting can be accessed at: 
https://acjcreporting.azcjc.gov/.
Office of Civil Rights Requirements: Annual Completion of Civil Rights Training is
required for this grant. Grantees must contact the Grant Coordinator to obtain registration
to access the training. If applicable, agencies awarded must complete an EEOC plan and
submit it to the Office of Civil Rights, Office of Justice Programs. Link to helpful EEOP
information: https://www.ojp.gov/program/civil-rights-office/home.
If you have any questions, please contact Siyeni Yitbarek at syitbarek@azcjc.gov or
602.364.1163. Our office looks forward to the continued partnership.
Sincerely,
Tony Vidale, Deputy Director
Drug, Gang, and Violent Crime Control Program
Our mission is to continuously address, improve, sustain, and enhance public safety in the State of Arizona through
the coordination, cohesiveness, and effectiveness of the Criminal Justice System.

This Grant Agreement is made this 29th day of May, 2024 by and between the ARIZONA CRIMINAL JUSTICE
COMMISSION hereinafter called "COMMISSION" and NAVAJO, through NAVAJO COUNTY SHERIFF'S OFFICE
hereinafter called GRANTEE. The COMMISSION enters into this Agreement pursuant to its authority under the
provisions of A.R.S. § 41-2405 (B)(6), and having satisfied itself as to the qualification of GRANTEE;
NOW, THEREFORE, it is agreed between the parties as follows:
1. This Agreement will commence on July 01, 2024 and terminate on June 30, 2025. This Agreement expires
at the end of the award period unless prior written approval for an extension has been obtained from the
COMMISSION. A request for an extension must be received by the COMMISSION sixty (60) days prior to the end
of the award period. The COMMISSION in its sole discretion may approve an extension that furthers the goals
and objectives of the program and shall determine the length of any extension.
2. GRANTEE agrees that grant funds will be used in accordance with applicable program rules, guidelines and
special conditions.
3. The COMMISSION will monitor GRANTEE performance against program goals and performance standards
and
those outlined in the grant application. Substandard performance as determined by the COMMISSION will
constitute noncompliance with this Agreement. If the COMMISSION finds noncompliance, the GRANTEE will
receive a written notice which identifies the area of noncompliance, and the appropriate corrective action to be
taken. If the GRANTEE does not respond within thirty (30) calendar days to this notice, and does not provide
sufficient information concerning the steps which are being taken to correct the problem, the COMMISSION may
suspend funding, permanently terminate this Agreement or revoke the grant.
4. Any deviation or failure to comply with the purpose and/or conditions of this Agreement without prior written
COMMISSION approval may constitute sufficient reason for the COMMISSION to terminate this Agreement,
revoke the grant, require the return of all unspent funds, perform an audit of expended funds, and require
the return of any previously spent funds which are deemed to have been spent in violation of the purpose or
conditions of this grant.
5. This Agreement may be modified only by a written amendment signed by the Executive Director or by persons
authorized by the Executive Director on behalf of the COMMISSION and GRANTEE. Any notice given pursuant to
this Agreement shall be in writing and shall be considered to have been given when actually received by the
following addressee or their agents or employees:

33. The parties agree to use arbitration in the event of disputes in accordance with the provisions of A.R.S. §
12-1501-12-1518. The laws of the State of Arizona apply to questions arising under this Agreement and any
litigation regarding this Agreement must be maintained in Arizona courts, except as pertaining to disputes
which are subject to arbitration.
I. In the event of a dispute, controversy, or claim arising out of or relating in any way to the Agreement, the
complaining Party shall notify the other Party in writing thereof. Within 30 calendar days of such notice,
representatives of both Parties shall meet at an agreed location to attempt to resolve the dispute in good
faith. Should the dispute not be resolved within 30 calendar days after such notice, the complaining Party
shall seek remedies exclusively through arbitration, in accordance with the provisions of A.R.S. § 12-1501
through 12-1518.
II. The arbitration demand must be a clear and concise statement of the claim or dispute. The respondent's
answer and any counterclaims must be filed within 20 calendar days of service of the demand.
III. The arbitration shall be conducted in Maricopa County.
IV. The arbitration shall be conducted by one arbitrator. If the Parties are not able to agree upon the selection
of an arbitrator, within 20 calendar days of the commencement of an arbitration proceeding by service of a
demand for arbitration, the court on application of a Party shall appoint the arbitrator.
V. The arbitrator must promptly set a conference to clarify the claims and defenses, to establish fair
procedures, and to set a schedule for completing the arbitration.
VI. It is the intent of the parties that, barring extraordinary circumstances, arbitration proceedings will be
concluded with 120 calendar days from the date the arbitrator is appointed. The arbitrator may extend the
time limit in the interests of justice. Failure to adhere to this time limit shall not constitute a basis for
challenging the award.

damages are allowed.
VIII. Unless the arbitrator orders otherwise, the Parties must share arbitration costs equally, including
arbitrator's fees and expenses. Each party must pay its own expenses and attorney's fees.

19. Unreasonable restrictions on competition und the award; association with federal government
SCOPE. This condition applies with respect to any procurement of property or services that is funded (in
whole or in part) by this award, regardless of the dollar amount of the purchase or acquisition, the method of
procurement, or the nature of any legal instrument used. The provisions of this condition must be among
those included in any award.
1. No discrimination, in procurement transactions, against associates of the federal government
Consistent with the (DOJ) Part 200 Uniform Requirements - including as set out at 2 C.F.R. 200.300
(requiring awards to be "manage[d] and administer[ed] in a manner so as to ensure that Federal funding
is expended and associated programs are implemented in full accordance with U.S. statutory and public
policy requirements") and 200.319(a) (generally requiring "[a]ll procurement transactions [to] be
conducted in a manner providing full and open cometition 'and forbidding practices "restrictive of
competition," such as "[p]lacing unreasonable requirements on firms in order for them to qualify to
do business' and taking "[a]ny arbitrary action in the procurement process") -no the basis of such
person or entity's status as an "associate of the federal government" (or on the basis of such person
or entity's status as a 200.319(a) or as specifically authorized by USDOJ.
2. Rules of construction
a. The term "associate of the federal government" means any person or entity engaged or employed
(in the past or at the present) by or on behalf of the federal government- as an employee, contractor or
subcontractor (at any tier), grant recipient or- subgrantee (at any tier), agenty, or otherwise- in
undertaking any work, project, or activity for or on behalf of (or in providing goods or services to or on
behalf of) the federal government, and includes any applicant for such employment or engagement, and
any person or entity committed by legal instrument to undertake any such work project, or activity (or
to provide such goods or services) in future.
b. Nothing in this condition shall be understood to authorize or require any grantee, any subgrantee at
any tier, or any person or other entity, to violate any federal law, including any applicable civil rights or
nondiscrimination law.

noncompetitive approach in any procurement contract that would exceed the Simplified Acquisition Threshold

Should any provision of a requirement of this award be held to be invalid or unenforceable by its terms, that

22. If the recipient is designated "high risk" by a federal grant-making agency currently or at any time during
the period of performance under this award, the GRANTEE must disclose that fact and certain related
information to the COMMISSION by e-mail at dcadmin@azcjc.gov. For purposes of this disclosure, high risk
includes any status under which a federal awarding agency provides additional oversight due to the
GRANTEE'S past performance, or other programmatic or financial concerns with the GRANTEE. The
GRANTEE'S disclosure must include the following: 1. The federal awarding agency that currently designates
the GRANTEE high risk, 2. The date the GRANTEE was designated high risk, 3. The high-risk point of
contact at that federal awarding agency (name, phone number, and e-mail address), and 4. The reasons for
the high-risk status as set out by the federal awarding agency.
23. Consonant with federal statutes that pertain to firearms and background checks -- including 18 U.S.C.
922 and 34 U.S.C. ch. 409 -- if the GRANTEE uses this award to fund (in whole or in part) a specific project
or program (such as a law enforcement, prosecution, or court program) that results in any court dispositions,
information, or other records that are "eligible records" (under federal or State law) relevant to the National
Instant Background Check System (NICS), or that has as one of its purposes the establishment or
improvement of records systems that contain any court dispositions, information, or other records that are
"eligible records" (under federal or State law) relevant to the NICS, the GRANTEE must ensure that all such
court dispositions, information, or other records that are "eligible records" (under federal or State law) relevant
to the NICS are promptly made available to the NICS or to the "State" repository/database that is
electronically available to (and accessed by) the NICS, and -- when appropriate -- promptly must update,
correct, modify, or remove such NICS relevant "eligible records". In the event of minor and transitory
non-compliance, the GRANTEE may submit evidence to demonstrate diligent monitoring of compliance with
this condition. COMMISSION will give great weight to any such evidence in any express written determination
regarding this condition.
24. GRANTEE acknowledges JAG funds may not be used as the 50% match for purposes of the DOJ
Bulletproof Vest Partnership (BVP) program.
25. GRANTEE must have written procedures in place to respond in the event of an actual or imminent
"breach" (OMB M-17-12) if it 1) creates, collects, uses, processes, stores, maintains, disseminates, discloses,
or disposes of "personally identifiable information (PII)" (2 CFR 200.79) within the scope of an OJP
grant-funded program or activity, or 2) uses or operates a "Federal information system" (OMB Circular A-130).
The GRANTEE'S breach procedures must include a requirement to report actual or imminent break of PII to
the COMMISSION no later than 24 hours after an occurrence of an actual breach, or the detection of an
imminent breach.
26. The GRANTEE warrants its compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations that relate to its
employees and A.R.S. § 23-214(A). If a grantee uses any subcontractors in performance of this
contract,subcontractors shall warrant their compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations that
relate to their employees and A.R.S. § 23-214(A). A breach of this warranty shall be deemed a material
breach of the contract subject to penalties up to and including termination of this contract. The Commission
retains the legal right to inspect the papers of the grantee and its subcontractors who work on the contract to
ensure that it or its subcontractors are complying with this warranty.

INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS
EXHIBIT "A"
Insurance Requirements for Governmental Parties to a Grant Agreement:
None.
Insurance Requirements for Any Contractors Used by a Party to the Grant Agreement:
(Note: this applies only to Contractors used by a governmental entity, not to the governmental entity itself.)
The insurance requirements herein are minimum requirements and in no way limit the indemnity covenants
contained in the Intergovernmental Agreement. The State of Arizona in no way warrants that the minimum
limits contained herein are sufficient to protect the governmental entity or Contractor from liabilities that might
arise out of the performance of the work under this Contract by the Contractor, his agents, representatives,
employees or subcontractors, and Contractor and the governmental entity are free to purchase additional
insurance.
A. MINIMUM SCOPE AND LIMITS OF INSURANCE: Contractor shall provide coverage with limits of liability
not less than those stated below.
1. Commercial General Liability - Occurrence Form
Policy shall include bodily injury, property damage, and broad form contractual liability.
a. The policy shall be endorsed, as required by this written agreement, to include the State of Arizona,
and its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, universities, officers, officials, agents, and
employees as additional insureds with respect to liability arising out of the activities performed by or
on behalf of the Contractor.
(Note that the other governmental entity(ies) is/are also required to be additional insured(s) and they
should supply the Contractor with their own list of persons to be insured.)
b. Policy shall contain a waiver of subrogation endorsement, as required by this written agreement, in
favor of the State of Arizona, and its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, universities,
officers, officials, agents, and employees for losses arising from work performed by or on behalf of the
Contractor.
2. Business Automobile Liability
Bodily Injury and Property Damage for any owned, hired, and/or non-owned vehicles used in the
performance of this Contract.
a. The policy shall be endorsed, as required by this written agreement, to include the State of Arizona,
and its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, universities, officers, officials, agents, and
employees as additional insureds with respect to liability arising out of the activities performed by
or on behalf of, the Contractor involving automobiles owned, hired and/or non-owned by the
Contractor.
b. Policy shall contain a waiver of subrogation endorsement as required by this written agreement in
favor of the State of Arizona, and its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, universities,
officers, officials, agents, and employees for losses arising from work performed by or on behalf of the
Contractor.
(Note that the other governmental entity(ies) is/are also required to be additional insured(s) and they
should supply the Contractor with their own list of persons to be insured.)
General Aggregate $2,000,000
Products – Completed Operations Aggregate $1,000,000
Personal and Advertising Injury $1,000,000
Fire Legal Liability $50,000
Each Occurrence $1,000,000
Combined Single Limit (CSL) $1,000,000

Additional Insurance Requirements:
The policies shall include, or be endorsed to include, as required by this written agreement, the following
provisions:
The Contractor's policies shall stipulate that the insurance afforded the Contractor shall be primary and that
any insurance carried by the Department, its agents, officials, employees or the State of Arizona shall be
excess and not contributory insurance, as provided by A.R.S. § 41-621 (E).
Insurance provided by the Contractor shall not limit the Contractor's liability assumed under the
indemnification provisions of this Contract.
Notice of Cancellation:
For each insurance policy required by the insurance provisions of this Contract, the Contractor must provide
to the State of Arizona, within two (2) business days of receipt, a notice if a policy is suspended, voided, or
cancelled for any reason. Such notice shall be mailed, emailed, hand delivered or sent by facsimile
transmission to (Enter Contracting Agency Representative's Name, Address, and Fax Number Here).
Acceptability of Insurers:
Contractor's insurance shall be placed with companies licensed in the State of Arizona or hold approved
non-admitted status on the Arizona Department of Insurance List of Qualified Unauthorized Insurers. Insurers
shall have an "A.M. Best" rating of not less than A- VII. The State of Arizona in no way warrants that the
above-required minimum insurer rating is sufficient to protect the Contractor from potential insurer insolvency.
Verification of Coverage:
Contractor shall furnish the State of Arizona with certificates of insurance (valid ACORD form or equivalent
approved by the State of Arizona) as required by this Contract. An authorized representative of the insurer
shall sign the certificates.
All certificates and endorsements, as required by this written agreement, are to be received and approved by
the State of Arizona before work commences. Each insurance policy required by this Contract must be in
effect at, or prior to, commencement of work under this Contract. Failure to maintain the insurance policies as
required by this Contract, or to provide evidence of renewal, is a material breach of contract.
All certificates required by this Contract shall be sent directly to the Department. The State of Arizona
project/contract number and project description shall be noted on the certificate of insurance. The State of
Arizona reserves the right to require complete copies of all insurance policies required by this Contract at any
time.

ELECTRONIC RECORD AND SIGNATURE DISCLOSURE  
From time to time, Arizona Criminal Justice Commission (we, us or Company) may be required 
by law to provide to you certain written notices or disclosures. Described below are the terms 
and conditions for providing to you such notices and disclosures electronically through the 
DocuSign system. Please read the information below carefully and thoroughly, and if you can 
access this information electronically to your satisfaction and agree to this Electronic Record and 
Signature Disclosure (ERSD), please confirm your agreement by selecting the check-box next to 
‘I agree to use electronic records and signatures’ before clicking ‘CONTINUE’ within the
DocuSign system. 
 
Getting paper copies  
At any time, you may request from us a paper copy of any record provided or made available 
electronically to you by us. You will have the ability to download and print documents we send 
to you through the DocuSign system during and immediately after the signing session and, if you 
elect to create a DocuSign account, you may access the documents for a limited period of time 
(usually 30 days) after such documents are first sent to you. After such time, if you wish for us to 
send you paper copies of any such documents from our office to you, you will be charged a 
$0.00 per-page fee. You may request delivery of such paper copies from us by following the 
procedure described below. 
 
Withdrawing your consent  
If you decide to receive notices and disclosures from us electronically, you may at any time 
change your mind and tell us that thereafter you want to receive required notices and disclosures 
only in paper format. How you must inform us of your decision to receive future notices and 
disclosure in paper format and withdraw your consent to receive notices and disclosures 
electronically is described below. 
 
Consequences of changing your mind  
If you elect to receive required notices and disclosures only in paper format, it will slow the 
speed at which we can complete certain steps in transactions with you and delivering services to 
you because we will need first to send the required notices or disclosures to you in paper format, 
and then wait until we receive back from you your acknowledgment of your receipt of such 
paper notices or disclosures. Further, you will no longer be able to use the DocuSign system to 
receive required notices and consents electronically from us or to sign electronically documents 
from us. 
 
All notices and disclosures will be sent to you electronically  
Electronic Record and Signature Disclosure created on: 3/5/2020 2:42:34 PM
Parties agreed to: Brad Carlyon, David Clouse, Wylie R Williams, Wylie R Williams

<!-- page 72 -->
Unless you tell us otherwise in accordance with the procedures described herein, we will provide 
electronically to you through the DocuSign system all required notices, disclosures, 
authorizations, acknowledgements, and other documents that are required to be provided or made 
available to you during the course of our relationship with you. To reduce the chance of you 
inadvertently not receiving any notice or disclosure, we prefer to provide all of the required 
notices and disclosures to you by the same method and to the same address that you have given 
us. Thus, you can receive all the disclosures and notices electronically or in paper format through 
the paper mail delivery system. If you do not agree with this process, please let us know as 
described below. Please also see the paragraph immediately above that describes the 
consequences of your electing not to receive delivery of the notices and disclosures 
electronically from us. 
 
How to contact Arizona Criminal Justice Commission:  
You may contact us to let us know of your changes as to how we may contact you electronically, 
to request paper copies of certain information from us, and to withdraw your prior consent to 
receive notices and disclosures electronically as follows: 
To contact us by email send messages to: alefevre@azcjc.gov 
 
To advise Arizona Criminal Justice Commission of your new email address  
To let us know of a change in your email address where we should send notices and disclosures 
electronically to you, you must send an email message to us at alefevre@azcjc.gov and in the 
body of such request you must state: your previous email address, your new email address.  We 
do not require any other information from you to change your email address.  
If you created a DocuSign account, you may update it with your new email address through your 
account preferences.  
 
To request paper copies from Arizona Criminal Justice Commission  
To request delivery from us of paper copies of the notices and disclosures previously provided 
by us to you electronically, you must send us an email to alefevre@azcjc.gov and in the body of 
such request you must state your email address, full name, mailing address, and telephone 
number. We will bill you for any fees at that time, if any. 
 
To withdraw your consent with Arizona Criminal Justice Commission  
To inform us that you no longer wish to receive future notices and disclosures in electronic 
format you may:

<!-- page 73 -->
i. decline to sign a document from within your signing session, and on the subsequent page, 
select the check-box indicating you wish to withdraw your consent, or you may; 
ii. send us an email to alefevre@azcjc.gov and in the body of such request you must state your 
email, full name, mailing address, and telephone number. We do not need any other information 
from you to withdraw consent..  The consequences of your withdrawing consent for online 
documents will be that transactions may take a longer time to process.. 
 
Required hardware and software  
The minimum system requirements for using the DocuSign system may change over time. The 
current system requirements are found here: https://support.docusign.com/guides/signer-guide-
signing-system-requirements.  
 
Acknowledging your access and consent to receive and sign documents electronically  
To confirm to us that you can access this information electronically, which will be similar to 
other electronic notices and disclosures that we will provide to you, please confirm that you have 
read this ERSD, and (i) that you are able to print on paper or electronically save this ERSD for

where you will be able to print on paper or save it for your future reference and access. Further,
if you consent to receiving notices and disclosures exclusively in electronic format as described 
herein, then select the check-box next to ‘I agree to use electronic records and signatures’ before
clicking ‘CONTINUE’ within the DocuSign system.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. i.        
Meeting Date: 07/09/2024  
Title: Equipment Ownership Transfer Agreement
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Equipment ownership transfer agreement between the Navajo County Sheriff's Office and Holbrook
Police Department for 20 portable radios and 20 mobile radios with accessories
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office was awarded L3Harris portable and mobile radios for distribution to
all Navajo County municipal police departments. This agreement allows for the transfer of 20 portable
radios and 20 mobile radios with accessories to Holbrook Police Department at no cost. This agreement
also relinquishes all rights, responsibilities and control of the equipment from the Navajo County Sheriff's
Office, making Holbrook Police Department the sole custodian of the equipment.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. j.        
Meeting Date: 07/09/2024  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement for Sun Valley Dispatch Services
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County Sheriff's Office and Sun Valley Fire District for
dispatch services
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office presently dispatches calls for fire services to Sun Valley Fire. This
updated agreement is effective July 1, 2024, and has been updated to conform to the current dispatch
per-call pricing across Navajo County, which is $30.00 per call invoiced quarterly. A call for service
shall be defined as any call where a field unit is sent to the location of an incident or to assist a citizen, or
any time a field unit initiates activity that would otherwise have resulted in the dispatch of a field unit.
Additionally, Sun Valley Fire shall be responsible for all radio equipment costs. Said equipment shall be
returned to Sun Valley Fire in the event this agreement is terminated for any reason.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. k.        
Meeting Date: 07/09/2024  
Title: Transfer of Property IGA Between Navajo County and Northland Pioneer College
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County, on behalf of the Sheriff's Office, and Northland
Pioneer College, on behalf of NALETA for NCSO to transfer one (1) virtual training simulator to
NALETA at no cost
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office was able to procure a VirTra virtual training simulator and related
equipment via monies appropriated from the peace officer training equipment fund ARS 41-1731 as a part
of Senate Bill 1720. The VirTra machine is and will continue to be housed at the Northern Arizona Law
Enforcement Training Academy. NCSO and NALETA have entered into the attached IGA transferring
all ownership responsibilities, maintenance, custody, and disposal requirements from Navajo County to
Northland Pioneer College, on behalf of their respective departments. This agreement becomes effective
when signed by authorized representatives of both parties.

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT BETWEEN NAVAJO
COUNTY, ON BEHALF OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY SHERIFF’S OFFICE
AND
NORTHLAND PIONEER COLLEGE, ON BEHALF OF THE NORTHERN
ARIZONA LAW ENFORCEMENT TRAINING ACADEMY
The parties to this Intergovernmental Agreement (“Agreement”) are Navajo County,
hereinafter “Navajo County,” on behalf of the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office, hereinafter
“NCSO” and Northland Pioneer College, hereinafter “NPC,” on behalf of the Northern Arizona
Law Enforcement Training Academy, hereinafter “NALETA.” (collectively the “Parties”)
AUTHORITY
Navajo County and NPC are authorized to enter into this Agreement pursuant to the
Provisions of Arizona Revised Statutes (“A.R.S.”) § 11-952 & § 11-251.
PURPOSE
The purpose of this agreement is for NCSO to transfer one (1) virtual training simulator,
described below, to NALETA at no cost.
SERVICES TO BE PROVIDED BY NAVAJO COUNTY
1. NCSO agrees to transfer to NALETA the following item(s):
a. 1 – VIR V-300LE-2 VirTra 300 LE (w/2-year warranty)
b. 1-VIR V-VICTA-01, 2-VIR V-M4-RK, 2-VIR V-M4-ASM, 1-OMNIA POP, 1-
VIR V-VICTA-01, 1-VIR V-300LE-2, 2-VIR SP-V300-A, 2-VIR VNLW-TAS-
X2, 1-VIR VS-V300-INSTL (all items as per specification on attached invoice)
2. Upon transfer, NCSO relinquishes all rights, responsibility, and control over the
equipment. Once the equipment is transferred, NALETA shall be the sole custodian of
the equipment and accepts all responsibility for the use of the equipment.  When of no
further use, NALETA shall be responsible for the disposal of the equipment.
SERVICES TO BE PROVIDED BY NPC & NALETA
1. NALETA agrees to be fully responsible for the custody, use, and maintenance of the
equipment after transfer.
FUNDING OBLIGATION
Neither NCSO nor NALETA may charge any administrative fees or costs of any kind for
any activities performed for property transferred pursuant to this agreement.

<!-- page 86 -->
Page  of 2 4
DURATION
This agreement becomes effective when signed by authorized representatives of both
parties.
NOTICE
Any written notice/communication provided for, required or permitted herein will be addressed
to the following:
Navajo County: NALETA:
County Manager  Director of Public Safety Education
PO Box 668  PO Box 1419
Holbrook, AZ 86025 Taylor, AZ 85939
INDEMNIFICATION
NALETA agrees to be fully responsible for the custody and use of the equipment listed
above, after transfer.  NALETA shall indemnify, defend, save and hold harmless NCSO from
and against any and all claims, actions, liabilities, damages, losses, or expenses, including court
costs, attorneys’ fees, and costs of claim processing, investigation, and litigation for any injuries
(including death), or loss of damage to tangible or intangible property arising from NALETA’s
possession or use of the equipment.
ARBITRATION
In the event of a dispute under the agreement, the parties agree to use arbitration to the
extent required under A.R.S. §12-1518 and A.R.S. §12-133.
NON-DISCRIMINATION
The parties agree to be bound by applicable State and federal rules governing Equal
Employment Opportunity and Non-Discrimination.
CONFLICT OF INTEREST
This Agreement is subject to cancellation pursuant to the provisions of A.R.S. §38-511
regarding Conflict of Interest.
AMENDMENTS
Any amendments to this Agreement must be in writing and signed by authorized
representatives of each party.

<!-- page 87 -->
Page  of 3 4
WAIVER
Waiver by either Party of any breach or default of any clause of this Agreement by the
other Party shall not operate as a waiver of any previous or future default or breach of the same
or different clause of this Agreement.
SEVERABILITY
If any provision of this Agreement is held void or unenforceable, the remaining
provisions shall nevertheless be effective, the intent being to effectuate this Agreement to the
fullest extent possible.
ENTIRE AGREEMENT
This Agreement embodies the entire understanding between the Navajo County and NPC
for this project, and any prior or contemporaneous representations, either oral or written are
hereby superseded.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the authorized representatives of the parties have executed this
agreement pursuant to resolutions duly adopted by their respective governing boards.

of the Intergovernmental Agreement between the City of Glendale and Navajo County for law
enforcement, security, and traffic management services at the State Farm Stadium; Contract with Robert
Santos for Law Enforcement Services assigned to Northern Arizona Law Enforcement Training Academy
(NALETA)
Background:
An IGA was established in January 2020, between the City of Glendale and Navajo County for law
enforcement, security, and traffic management services at the State Farm Stadium. The IGA was extended
in July 2021, and is again set to expire on June 30, 2023. Contract extension 2 will be effective July 1,
2024, and will expire June 30, 2027. The original contract and extension 1 are also attached.
Contract for Law Enforcement Services for Navajo County Sheriff's Office with Robert Anthony Santos
whom will be assigned to NALETA.

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT
BETWEEN
CITY OF GLENDALE AND NAVAJO COUNTY
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT ( this " Agreement") is entered into pursuant to
Arizona Revised Statutes(" A. R. S.")§ 11- 952 by the City of Glendale(" Glendale") and Navajo County(" Navajo
County"), collectively known herein as the" parties."
RECITALS
A.       The State Farm Stadium in Glendale( the" Stadium") is located within Glendale' s corporate limits and
Glendalehasjurisdictionalauthorityover andpublic safetyinterestsin the operationof the Stadium;
and
B.       The City of Glendalehasthe needfor qualifiedpersonnelto provide law enforcement, security, and
traffic management services at the Stadium; and
C.       The parties desireto participatein providing law enforcement, security, and traffic management
servicesto the City of Glendale,relatingto the Stadium,by makingavailableNavajoCounty Sheriff's
Office(" NCSO") and Glendale Police Department(" GPD") officers in accordance with the terms set
forth herein.
D.       Thepartiesdesireto enterinto thisAgreementto cooperativelyprovidethenecessarylawenforcement,
security, andtraffic managementservices,while acknowledgingGlendale's primary responsibilityfor
law enforcement at the Stadium.
AGREEMENT
Therefore, in considerationof the foregoingrecitals, the covenants, promises, terms and conditions
set forth herein, and other good and valuableconsideration, the receipt and adequacyof which is hereby
acknowledged, the parties hereby agreeas follows:
1. Purpose and Intent. The purpose of this Agreement is to provide the means through which the
partiesintend to provide professionallaw enforcement, security, and traffic managementservicesat
theStadiumby maximizingcooperation,by integratingthe NCSO andGPD assets,andby addressing
issuesincluding,command,control, personnel,planning,andtraining.
2.       Supervision and Staffing.
2. 1 Navajo County acknowledges that command and control for all events worked for pursuant
to this Agreement ( individually " Event" and collectively " Events") shall be the duty and
responsibility of GPD.
2. 2 In carrying out this responsibility, GPD will, in good faith assign officers to work Events in
accordance with the procedures adopted in consultation with the Chiefs of Police for all the
member agenciesproviding officers to Events.
Page 1 of 7

<!-- page 92 -->
2. 3 Navajo County agreesandunderstandsthat entitiesother than Glendaleand the GPD may
have input into the decisions regarding whether, and to what extent, law enforcement will
provide service for Events; however, Glendale will provide information regarding staffing
decisions to Navajo County as soon asit becomes available.
3.       Assigned Personnel
3. 1 NavajoCounty shallhavethe discretionto determinewhich andhow manyof its officerswill
be allowedto applyfor assignmentsat Eventsandhow manyofficerswill be assignedto the
Events ( hereinafter referred to as" Assigned Officers").
a. All Assigned Officers will be required to enter into temporary employment contracts
with Glendale.
b.       The contract will outline the mutual responsibilities of the Assigned Officer and
Glendale and will specify that the Assigned Officers are " at will" temporary
employees of Glendale and that either party can terminate the contract, with or
without cause at any time and without notice.
c. If possible, Glendale will consult with Navajo County prior to terminating any
Assigned Officer.
3. 2 While working anEvent,theAssignedOfficers mustwearuniformsapprovedby NCSO,and
the AssignedOfficers maycarryother equipmentauthorizedby NCSO.
3. 3 When working an Event, GPD will make available to Assigned Officers forms and other
supplies that are necessaryto work the Event.
3. 4 Upon terminationof thisAgreementandconclusionof anyassignments,allpersonalproperty,
assets,equipment, and supplies used by the parties and Assigned Officers in performance of
their responsibilities shall remain with or be returned to the owner of such property.
3. 5 Any inconsistencybetweenGPD andNCSO rulesor regulationsandall operationaldisputes
will immediatelybebroughtto the attentionof theGlendalePoliceChiefandwill befully and
finally addressedand resolvedby the GlendalePolice Chief in accordancewith his or her
determinationof the bestpracticesunderthe circumstances. The GlendalePoliceChief may
delegate this responsibility to a specific command officer.
4.       Compensation, Insurance and Reporting
4. 1 The partiesagreethat during Eventsthe AssignedOfficers shallbe temporaryemployeesof
Glendale for compensation purposes and not independent contractors; provided however,
that each such Assigned Officer will at all times also be deemed an employee of NCSO and
nothing in this Agreement is intended to contradict or otherwise modify the provisions of
A. R. S. § 23- 1022 ( D).
4. 2 EachAssignedOfficer who works anEvent will bepaid the negotiatedhourly rate.
a. Assigned Officers' compensation shall be subject to all applicable federal and state
taxes,which shallbe deductedprior to payment, andwhich shallbe evidencedby a
W-2 statementissuedby Glendaleto eachAssignedOfficer.
Page 2 of 7

<!-- page 93 -->
b.       Each officer' s home agency  ( primary employer)  shall provide the workers'
compensation coverage in such amounts and under the same terms and conditions as
other sworn, full- time employees for all Assigned Officers.
c. Exceptfor liability coverage,AssignedOfficersarenot entitledto anyotheremployee
benefits or compensation from Glendale.
4. 3 Glendale shall make available, upon request, to the NCSO information about the hours
workedby AssignedOfficers not laterthan sevendaysfollowing eachEvent to enableNCSO
to properlymonitor andregulatethe hoursworkedby all of their AssignedOfficers.
4. 4 Each party affirms that it has complied with the provisions of A. R. S. § 23- 1022 ( E) with
respect to activities addressedby the Agreement.
5.       Indemnification
5. 1 To the extent permitted by law, Glendaleshallindemnify, defend, saveand hold harmless
NavajoCounty,its departments,agencies,boards,commissions,officers,officials,agents,and
employees ( hereinafter referred to as " Indemnitee") for, from andagainstanyandall claims,
actions, liabilities, damages, losses, or expenses (including court costs, attorneys' fees, and
costs of claim processing, investigation and litigation) ( hereinafter referred to as" Claims") for
bodily injury or personalinjury (includingdeath),or lossor damageto tangibleor intangible
property caused,or allegedto be caused,in whole or in part, by the negligentactsor willful
misconduct of the Assigned Officers.

Indemniteeto conform to anyfederal,stateor locallaw,statute,ordinance,rule,regulationor

judgment costs where this indemnification is applicable and in consideration of the covenants
and promises set forth in this Agreement, Glendale agrees to waive all rights of subrogation
against Navajo County, its officers, officials, agents and employees for lossesarising from the
work performedby NavajoCounty'sAssignedOfficers underthis Agreement.
6.       Media Releases and Relations
6. 1 Any releaseof information to the media, other than a public recordsrelease, regardingan
Event or anyactivitiesunderthis Agreementwill be coordinatedby Glendalein cooperation
with Navajo County but, except as provided below, no unilateral media releases will be
distributedby NavajoCountywithout the prior approvalof Glendale.
6. 2 A copy of all public record and media releasesregarding an Event or any activities under this
Agreement shall be forwarded to Glendale prior to release; provided however, if an incident
is primarily focusedupon or concernedwith the actionsof NCSO'sAssignedOfficer, NCSO
will be responsible for the release of information to the media relative to the incident.
6. 3 The parties will not reveal any investigative information or operational procedures except as
required by law.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. m.        
Meeting Date: 07/09/2024  
Title: Mutual Aid Agreement with Coconino County Sheriff's Office
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Mutual Aid Agreement between the Coconino County Sheriff's Office and the Navajo County Sheriff's
Office for law enforcement assistance
Background:
Due to an existing need in the southeastern jurisdiction of the Coconino County Sheriff's Office for law
enforcement assistance, the Coconino County Sheriff's Office and the Navajo County Sheriff's Office
wish to enter into a mutual aid agreement. The attached agreement establishes the terms and conditions
under which NCSO will provide law enforcement assistance in southeastern Coconino County. This
agreement provides for an $87 response per call for service compensation, with certain criteria being met,
with quarterly reimbursement. This IGA shall commence upon approval of the parties and expires
December 31, 2026.
Attachments
Coconino County Sheriff's Office Mutual Aid Agreement
